/// back-off and a dead-letter fallback.\n    /// </summary>\n    public sealed class EventPublisher\n    {\n        private const string DefaultTopic = \"etl-domain-events\";\n\n        private readonly IKafkaProducer<string, byte[]> _producer;\n        private readonly IDeadLetterSink _deadLetterSink;\n        private readonly AsyncRetryPolicy _retryPolicy;\n\n        public EventPublisher(\n            IKafkaProducer<string, byte[]> producer,\n            IDeadLetterSink deadLetterSink,\n            int retryCount = 3)\n        {\n            _producer = producer ?? throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(producer));\n            _deadLetterSink = deadLetterSink ?? throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(deadLetterSink));\n\n            _retryPolicy = Policy\n                .Handle<Exception>() // Capture any broker/transport exceptions.\n                .WaitAndRetryAsync(\n                    retryCount,\n                    retryAttempt => TimeSpan.FromMilliseconds(Math.Pow(2, retryAttempt) * 100),\n                    onRetry: (ex, ts, i, _) =>\n                    {\n                        // In production we'd emit a structured log     public async Task PublishAsync(IDomainEvent domainEvent, CancellationToken ct = default)\n        {\n            if (domainEvent == null) throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(domainEvent));\n\n            var serialized = JsonSerializer.SerializeToUtf8Bytes(domainEvent, domainEvent.GetType());\n\n            try\n            {\n                await _retryPolicy.ExecuteAsync(\n                    async token => await _producer.ProduceAsync(\n                        DefaultTopic,\n                        domainEvent.EventId.ToString(\"N\"),\n                        serialized,\n                        token),\n                    ct);\n            }\n            catch (Exception finalEx)\n            {\n                // Fallback to dead-letter sink.\n                await _deadLetterSink.SendAsync(serialized, ct);\n\n                // Bubble up so that calling code can still be aware something went wrong.\n                throw new EventPublishException(\n                    $\"Failed to publish event {domainEvent.EventId}\", finalEx);\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Exception thrown when an event could not be published.\n    /// Thread-safe, minimalistic and deterministic.\n    /// </summary>\n    internal sealed class InMemoryEventBus : IEventBus\n    {\n        private readonly ConcurrentDictionary<Type, List<Delegate>> _subscriptions = new();\n\n        public void Publish<TEvent>(TEvent @event) where TEvent : IPipelineEvent\n        {\n            if (_subscriptions.TryGetValue(typeof(TEvent), out var handlers))\n            {\n                // Clone to avoid concurrency issues while enumerating.\n                var snapshot = handlers.ToArray();\n                foreach (var handler in snapshot.Cast<Action<TEvent>>())\n                {\n                    handler(@event);\n                }\n            }\n        }\n\n        public void Subscribe<TEvent>(Action<TEvent> handler) where TEvent : IPipelineEvent\n        {\n            _subscriptions.AddOrUpdate(\n                typeof(TEvent),\n                _ => new List<Delegate> { handler },\n                (_, list) =>\n                {\n                    list.Add(handler);\n                    return list;\n                });\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    ///  Simple pipeline orchestrator that executes stages sequentially and surfaces events via IEventBus.\n    /// </summary>\n    internal sealed class EtlPipeline\n    {\n        private readonly IEnumerable<IPipelineStage> _stages;\n        private readonly IEventBus _eventBus;\n\n        public EtlPipeline(IEnumerable<IPipelineStage> stages, IEventBus eventBus)\n        {\n            _stages   = stages ?? throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(stages));\n            _eventBus = eventBus ?? throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(eventBus));\n        }\n\n        public async Task<DataPigment> ExecuteAsync(DataPigment pigment, CancellationToken ct = default)\n        {\n            _eventBus.Publish(new IngestedEvent(pigment.Id, DateTimeOffset.UtcNow));\n\n            var current = pigment;\n            foreach (var stage in _stages)\n            {\n                try\n                {\n                    current = await stage.ProcessAsync(current, ct).ConfigureAwait(false);\n                    _eventBus.Publish(new TransformedEvent(current.Id, DateTimeOffset.UtcNow));\n                }\n                catch (Exception ex)\n                {\n                    _eventBus.Publish(new ErrorEvent(current.Id, DateTimeOffset.UtcNow, stage.GetType().Name, ex));\n                    throw; // let the orchestrator bubble the exception so the caller can react.\n                }\n            }\n\n            _eventBus.Publish(new LoadedEvent(current.Id, DateTimeOffset.UtcNow));\n            return current;\n        }\n    }\n\n    #endregion\n\n    #region ‑-[ Tests ]--\n\n    public sealed class DataFlowAndEventContractTests\n    {\n        private static DataPigment GeneratePigment() =>\n            new(Guid.NewGuid(), new observedEvents.Add(e));\n            bus.Subscribe<LoadedEvent>(e      => observedEvents.Add(e));\n\n            // Two trivial mock stages\n            var stage1 = new Mock<IPipelineStage>();\n CancellationToken _) =>\n                      p with { Payload = p.Payload.Append(\"stage1\", \"ok\") });\n\n            stage2.Setup(s => s.ProcessAsync(It.IsAny<DataPigment>(), It.IsAny<CancellationToken>()))\n                  .ReturnsAsync((DataPigment p, CancellationToken _) =>\n                      p with { Payload = p.Payload.Append(\"stage2\", \"ok\") });\n\n \"Ingested\",\n                TransformedEvent=> \"Transformed\",\n                LoadedEvent     => \"Loaded\",\n                _               => \"Unknown\"\n            })\n concurrent load\")]\n        public async Task EventBusDispatchesToAllSubscribersExactlyOnce()\n        {\n            // Arrange ----------------------------------------------------------\n            var bus = new InMemoryEventBus();\n\n            var ingestedCount  = 0;\n            var transformedCount = 0;\n            var loadedCount    = 0;\n\n            bus.Subscribe<IngestedEvent>(_ => Interlocked.Increment(ref ingestedCount));\n            bus.Subscribe<TransformedEvent>(_ => Interlocked.Increment(ref transformedCount));\n            bus.Subscribe<LoadedEvent>(_ => Interlocked.Increment(ref loadedCount));\n\n            var stage = new Mock<IPipelineStage>();\n            stage.Setup(s => s.ProcessAsync(It.IsAny<DataPigment>(), It.IsAny<CancellationToken>()))\n                 .ReturnsAsync((DataPigment p, CancellationToken _) => p);\n\n            var pipeline = new EtlPipeline(Enumerable.Repeat(stage.Object, 3), bus);\n\n            // Act        {\n            _logger.LogError(ex,\n                \"Unhandled exception while processing envelope {EnvelopeId}\",\n                envelope.Metadata.EnvelopeId);\n\n            await _sink.PublishDeadLetterAsync(envelope, ex, token);\n\n            await BroadcastAsync(o => o.OnTransformationFailedAsync(envelope, ex, token), token);\n        }\n\n        private async Task BroadcastAsync(\n            Func<ITransformationObserver, Task> notify,\n            CancellationToken token)\n        {\n            foreach (var observer in _observers)\n            {\n                try\n                {\n                    if (token.IsCancellationRequested) return;\n                    await notify(observer);\n                }\n                catch (Exception ex)\n                {\n                    // Observers should never bring down the worker\n                    _logger.LogWarning(ex,\n                        \"Observer {Observer} threw during notification. Continuing...\",\n                        observer.GetType().Name);\n                }\n            }\n        }\n\n        #endregion\n    }\n\n    #region Contracts / Supporting Models\n\n    /// _eventBus.PublishAsync(result, ct).ConfigureAwait(false);\n            }\n            catch (Exception ex)\n            {\n                _logger.LogError(ex,\n                    \"Failed to persist/publish quality check result {CheckName}. \" +\n                    \"The result will not be lost, but metrics may be inaccurate.\", result.CheckName);\n            }\n        }\n\n        private async Task<QualityCheckResult> ExecuteCheckSafeAsync(\n            IQualityCheck check,\n            DataBatch batch,\n            CancellationToken ct)\n        {\n            var sw = Stopwatch.StartNew();\n            try\n            {\n                var result = await check.ExecuteAsync(batch, ct).ConfigureAwait(false);\n                sw.Stop();\n\n                return new QualityCheckResult(\n                    checkName: check.Name,\n                    status: result.Status,\n                    duration: sw.Elapsed,\n                    details: result.Details);\n            }\n            catch (OperationCanceledException) when (ct.IsCancellationRequested)\n            {\n                // Propagate cancellation\n                throw;\n            }\n            catch (Exception ex)\n            {\n                sw.Stop();\n                _logger.LogWarning(ex, \"Quality check {CheckName} encountered an error\", check.Name);\n\n                return new QualityCheckResult(\n                    checkName: check.Name,\n                    status: QualityCheckStatus.Error,\n                    duration: sw.Elapsed,\n                    error: ex,\n                    details: ex.Message);\n            }\n        }\n\n        #endregion\n    }\n\n    #region Example Checks (for completeness only)\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// A dummy check that always passes. These exemplars would normally live in their own files.\n    /// </summary>\n    public sealed class AlwaysGreenCheck : IQualityCheck\n    {\n        public string Name => \"always_green\";\n\n        public Task<QualityCheckResult> ExecuteAsync(DataBatch batch, CancellationToken ct)\n        {\n            return Task.FromResult(new QualityCheckResult(\n                checkName: Name,\n                status: QualityCheckStatus.Passed,\n                duration: TimeSpan.Zero,\n                details: \"Everything looks good!\"));\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Simulates a null-percentage check on a column; fails if threshold exceeds value provided in metadata.\n    /// </summary>\n    public sealed class NullRatioCheck : IQualityCheck\n    {\n        public const string RatioKey = \"null_ratio\";\n\n        public string Name => \"null_ratio\";\n\n        public Task<QualityCheckResult> ExecuteAsync(DataBatch batch, CancellationToken ct)\n        {\n            // In real implementation, we'd inspect data frame here.\n            if (!batch.Metadata.TryGetValue(RatioKey, out var ratioObj) ||\n                ratioObj is not double ratio)\n            {\n                return Task.FromResult(new QualityCheckResult(\n                    Name,\n                    QualityCheckStatus.Skipped,\n                    TimeSpan.Zero,\n                    details: \"No null ratio metadata supplied\"));\n            }\n\n            var status = ratio <= 0.01 ? QualityCheckStatus.Passed : QualityCheckStatus.Failed;\n            var details = $\"Null ratio: {ratio:P2}\";\n\n            return Task.FromResult(new QualityCheckResult(\n                Name,\n                status,\n                TimeSpan.Zero,\n                details: details));\n        }\n    }\n\n    #endregion\n}\n```","numLines":331,"startLine":1,"totalLines":331}}}

Retrying in {Delay}.\",\n                            i, _settings.RetryCount, ts);\n                    });\n        }\n\n        #region Public API\n\n        /// <inheritdoc />\n        public async Task UploadAsync(\n            string zone,\n            string blobPath,\n            Stream content,\n            bool overwrite            = false,\n            CancellationToken cancel  = default)\n        {\n            ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull(zone);\n            ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull(blobPath);\n            ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull(content);\n\n            await _retryPolicy.ExecuteAsync(async ct =>\n            {\n                var fileClient = await GetFileClientAsync(zone, blobPath, ct).ConfigureAwait(false);\n\n                content.Position = 0;\n                await fileClient.UploadAsync(content, overwrite, cancellationToken: ct).ConfigureAwait(false);\n\n                _logger.LogInformation(\n                    \"Uploaded file to Data Lake. Zone: {Zone}, Path: {Path}, Overwrite: {Overwrite}\",\n                    zone, blobPath, overwrite);\n            }, cancel).ConfigureAwait(false);\n        }\n\n        /// <inheritdoc />\n        public async Task<Stream> DownloadAsync(\n            string zone,\n            string blobPath,\n            CancellationToken cancel = default)\n        {\n            ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull(zone);\n            ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull(blobPath);\n\n            return await _retryPolicy.ExecuteAsync(async ct =>\n            {\n                var fileClient = await GetFileClientAsync(zone, blobPath, ct).ConfigureAwait(false);\n\n                Response<FileDownloadInfo> response = await fileClient.ReadAsync(ct).ConfigureAwait(false);\n                _logger.LogInformation(\n                    \"Downloaded file from Data Lake. Zone: {Zone}, Path: {Path}, Size: {Size} B\",\n                    zone, blobPath, response.Value.ContentLength);\n\n                // Caller is responsible for disposing the stream.\n                return response.Value.Content;\n            }, cancel).ConfigureAwait(false);\n        }\n\n        /// <inheritdoc />\n        public async Task<bool> ExistsAsync(\n            string zone,\n            string blobPath,\n            CancellationToken cancel = default)\n        {\n            ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull(zone);\n            ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull(blobPath);\n\n            return await _retryPolicy.ExecuteAsync(async ct =>\n            {\n                var fileClient = await GetFileClientAsync(zone, blobPath, ct).ConfigureAwait(false);\n                bool exists = await fileClient.ExistsAsync(ct).ConfigureAwait(false);\n                _logger.LogDebug(\"File {Path} in zone {Zone} exists: {Exists}\", blobPath, zone, exists);\n                return     string destPath,\n            bool overwrite           = false,\n            CancellationToken cancel = default)\n        {\n            ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull(zone);\n            ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull(sourcePath);\n            ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull(destPath);\n\n            await _retryPolicy.ExecuteAsync(async ct =>\n            {\n                var fileSystem   = await GetFileSystemAsync(zone, ct).ConfigureAwait(false);\n                var sourceClient = fileSystem.GetFileClient(sourcePath);\n                var destClient   = fileSystem.GetFileClient(destPath);\n\n                if (overwrite && await destClient.ExistsAsync(ct).ConfigureAwait(false))\n                {\n                    await Zone: {Zone}, From: {Source}, To: {Destination}\",\n                    zone, sourcePath, destPath);\n            }, cancel).ConfigureAwait(false);\n        }\n\n        /// <inheritdoc />\n        public async Task<string> GenerateSasAsync(\n            string zone,\n            string blobPath,\n            DateTimeOffset expiresOn,\n            CancellationToken cancel = default)\n        {\n            ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull(zone);\n            ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull(blobPath);\n\n            var fileClient = await GetFileClientAsync(zone, blobPath, cancel).ConfigureAwait(false);\n\n            DataLakeSasBuilder sas = new(\n                fileSystemName: fileClient.FileSystemName,\n                path:           fileClient.Path)\n            {\n                ExpiresOn = expiresOn,\n            };\n\n            sas.SetPermissions(DataLakeSasPermissions.Read | DataLakeSasPermissions.List);\n\n            string uri = fileClient.GenerateSasUri(sas).ToString();\n            _logger.LogDebug(\"Generated SAS URI for {Path} in zone {Zone}\", blobPath, zone);\n\n            return uri;\n        }\n\n        #endregion\n\n        #region Private helpers\n\n        private async Task<DataLakeFileClient> GetFileClientAsync(\n            string zone,\n            string blobPath,\n            CancellationToken cancel)\n        {\n            var fileSystem = await GetFileSystemAsync(zone, cancel).ConfigureAwait(false);\n            return fileSystem.GetFileClient(blobPath);\n        }\n\n        private async Task<DataLakeFileSystemClient> GetFileSystemAsync(\n            string zone,\n            CancellationToken cancel)\n        {\n            var fileSystem = _serviceClient.GetFileSystemClient(zone.ToLowerInvariant());\n\n            // Lazily create if not exists. It maintains an internal\n    /// bounded channel to enable back-pressure and leverages a configurable degree of\n    /// parallelism to efficiently saturate available CPU / IO resources.\n    /// </summary>\n    public sealed class LoaderWorker : BackgroundService\n    {\n        private readonly IIngestionQueue _ingestionQueue;\n        private readonly IServiceProvider _serviceProvider;\n        private readonly ILogger<LoaderWorker> _logger;\n        private readonly LoaderOptions _options;\n        private readonly Channel<PigmentMessage> _channel;\n        private readonly ILoadMetricsCollector _metrics;\n\n        public LoaderWorker(\n            IIngestionQueue ingestionQueue,\n            IServiceProvider serviceProvider,\n            IOptions<LoaderOptions> options,\n            ILoadMetricsCollector metrics,\n            ILogger<LoaderWorker> logger)\n        {\n            _ingestionQueue = ingestionQueue ?? throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(ingestionQueue));\n            _serviceProvider = serviceProvider ?? throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(serviceProvider));\n            _logger = logger ?? throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(logger));\n            _options = options?.Value ?? throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(options));\n            _metrics = metrics ?? throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(metrics));\n\n            _channel = Channel.CreateBounded<PigmentMessage>(new BoundedChannelOptions(_options.ChannelCapacity)\n            {\n                SingleReader = false,\n                SingleWriter = true,\n                FullMode = BoundedChannelFullMode.Wait\n            });\n        }\n\n        protected override async Task ExecuteAsync(CancellationToken stoppingToken)\n        {\n            _logger.LogInformation(\"LoaderWorker started with {Parallelism} parallelism.\", _options.MaxDegreeOfParallelism);\n\n            // Fire-and-forget pump from queue -> channel.\n            _ = Task.Run(() => PumpQueueAsync(stoppingToken), stoppingToken);\n\n            // Spin up consumer tasks\n            var consumers = new List<Task>();\n            for (var i = 0; i < _options.MaxDegreeOfParallelism; i++)\n            {\n                consumers.Add(Task.Run(() => ConsumeChannelAsync(i, stoppingToken), stoppingToken));\n            }\n\n            await Task.WhenAll(consumers);\n        }\n\n        #region Producer\n\n        private async Task PumpQueueAsync(CancellationToken ct)\n        {\n            try\n            {\n                while (!ct.IsCancellationRequested)\n                {\n                    var msg = await _ingestionQueue.DequeueAsync(ct).ConfigureAwait(false);\n                    await _channel.Writer.WriteAsync(msg, ct).ConfigureAwait(false);\n                }\n            }\n            catch (OperationCanceledException)\n            {\n                // Expected on shutdown.\n            }\n            catch (Exception ex)\n            {\n                _logger.LogCritical(ex, \"[Pump] Fatal error while reading from ingestion queue.\");\n            }\n            finally\n            {\n                _channel.Writer.TryComplete();\n            }\n        }\n\n        #endregion\n\n        #region Consumer\n\n        private async Task ConsumeChannelAsync(int workerId, CancellationToken ct)\n        {\n            await foreach (var msg in _channel.Reader.ReadAllAsync(ct).ConfigureAwait(false))\n            {\n                var sw = Stopwatch.StartNew();\n                try\n                {\n                    using var scope = _serviceProvider.CreateScope();\n                    var strategyFactory = scope.ServiceProvider.GetRequiredService<ILoaderStrategyFactory>();\n                    var strategy = strategyFactory.GetStrategy(msg.Format);\n\n                    _logger.LogDebug(\"[{Worker}] Loading pigment {PigmentId} via {Strategy}.\", workerId, msg.Id, strategy.GetType().Name);\n\n                    await strategy.LoadAsync(msg, ct).ConfigureAwait(false);\n\n                    _metrics.RecordSuccess(msg.SourceSystem, sw.Elapsed);\n                    _logger.LogInformation(\"[{Worker}] Successfully loaded pigment {PigmentId} in {Elapsed}.\",\n                        workerId, msg.Id, sw.Elapsed);\n                }\n                catch (OperationCanceledException)\n                {\n                    // Graceful shutdown or individual timeout.\n                    _metrics.RecordCancellation(msg.SourceSystem);\n                    _logger.LogWarning(\"[{Worker}] Loading pigment {PigmentId} cancelled.\", workerId, msg.Id);\n                }\n                catch (Exception ex)\n                {\n                    _metrics.RecordFailure(msg.SourceSystem, ex);\n                    _logger.LogError(ex, \"[{Worker}] Failed to load pigment {PigmentId}.\", workerId, msg.Id);\n\n                    if (_options.RetryFailedMessages)\n                    {\n                        // Push to dead-letter or retry queue depending on policy.\n                        await _ingestionQueue.RequeueAsync(msg, ct).ConfigureAwait(false);\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n        }\n\n        #endregion\n    }\n\n    #region Options & Models\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Configurable settings for <see cref=\"LoaderWorker\"/>.\n    /// </summary>\n    public sealed class LoaderOptions\n    {\n        /// <summary>\n        /// Max concurrent loader tasks.\n        /// </summary>\n        public int MaxDegreeOfParallelism { get; set; } = Environment.ProcessorCount;\n\n        /// <summary>\n        /// be re-queued for retry.\n        /// </summary>\n        public bool RetryFailedMessages { get; set; } = true;\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Represents a unit of raw data flowing through the init; } = Guid.NewGuid();\n\n        public DateTimeOffset Timestamp { get; }\n\n        public IReadOnlyDictionary<string, object?> Columns { get; }\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Public contract for a pipeline that can be executed.\n    /// </summary>\n    public interface IPipeline\n    {\n        /// <summary>\n        /// Runs the pipeline.\n        /// </summary>\n        /// <param name=\"source\">Initial input stream.</param>\n        /// <param name=\"cancellationToken\">Cancellation token.</param>\n        /// <returns>Transformed output stream.</returns>\n        Task<IAsyncEnumerable<PigmentFrame>> ExecuteAsync(\n            IAsyncEnumerable<PigmentFrame> source,\n            CancellationToken cancellationToken = default);\n    }\n\n    #endregion\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Implements builder pattern for composing complex transformation pipelines.\n    /// </summary>\n    public sealed class PipelineBuilder\n    {\n        private readonly List<Func<IServiceProvider, IPipelineStage>> _factoryDelegates = new();\n        private readonly List<ITransformationObserver> _observers = new();\n        private readonly IServiceProvider _services;\n        private readonly ILogger<PipelineBuilder> _logger;\n        private bool _built;\n\n        public PipelineBuilder(IServiceProvider services)\n        {\n            _services = services ?? throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(services));\n            _logger = _services.GetRequiredService<ILogger<PipelineBuilder>>();\n        }\n\n        /// <summary>\n        /// Adds a new stage to the pipeline. The stage is resolved from the container.\n        /// </summary>\n        public PipelineBuilder AddStage<TStage>() where TStage : class, IPipelineStage\n        {\n            EnsureNotBuilt();\n            _factoryDelegates.Add(sp => sp.GetRequiredService<TStage>());\n            _logger.LogDebug(\"Queued stage {StageType}\", typeof(TStage).Name);\n            return this;\n        }\n\n        /// <summary>\n        /// Adds a stage using a custom factory delegate.\n        /// </summary>\n        public PipelineBuilder AddStage<TStage>(Func<IServiceProvider, TStage> factory)\n            where TStage : class, IPipelineStage\n        {\n            if (factory is null)\n                throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(factory));\n\n            EnsureNotBuilt();\n            _factoryDelegates.Add(sp => factory(sp));\n            _logger.LogDebug(\"Queued stage via factory {StageType}\", typeof(TStage).Name);\n            return this;\n        }\n\n        /// <summary>\n        /// Registers an observer which receives lifecycle callbacks.\n        /// </summary>\n        public PipelineBuilder AddObserver(ITransformationObserver observer)\n        {\n            if (observer is null)\n                throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(observer));\n\n            EnsureNotBuilt();\n            _observers.Add(observer);\n            return this;\n        }\n\n        /// <summary>\n        /// Builds an immutable pipeline instance. This method is idempotent,\n        /// subsequent calls return the same instance.\n        /// </summary>\n        public IPipeline Build()\n        {\n            if (_built)\n                throw new InvalidOperationException(\"Pipeline has already been built.\");\n\n            _built = true;\n\n            // Materialise stages at build-time.\n            var stages = _factoryDelegates.Select(f => f(_services)).ToArray();\n\n            if (stages.Length == 0)\n                throw new InvalidOperationException(\"Cannot build an empty pipeline.\");\n\n            return new BuiltPipeline(\n                stages,\n                _observers.ToArray(),\n                _services.GetRequiredService<ILogger<BuiltPipeline>>());\n        }\n\n        #region Private Helpers\n        private void EnsureNotBuilt()\n        {\n            if (_built)\n                throw new InvalidOperationException(\"Pipeline has already been built and cannot be modified.\");\n        }\n        #endregion\n\n        #region Inner Classes\n\n        /// <summary>\n        /// Concrete pipeline produced by the <see cref=\"PipelineBuilder\"/>.\n        /// </summary>\n        private sealed class BuiltPipeline : IPipeline\n        {\n            private readonly IReadOnlyList<IPipelineStage> _stages;\n            private readonly IReadOnlyList<ITransformationObserver> _observers;\n            private readonly ILogger<BuiltPipeline> _logger;\n\n            public BuiltPipeline(\n                IReadOnlyList<IPipelineStage> stages,\n                IReadOnlyList<ITransformationObserver> observers,\n                ILogger<BuiltPipeline> logger)\n            {\n                _stages = stages;\n                _observers = observers;\n                _logger = logger;\n            }\n\n            public Task<IAsyncEnumerable<PigmentFrame>> ExecuteAsync(\n                IAsyncEnumerable<PigmentFrame> source,\n                CancellationToken cancellationToken = default)\n            {\n                if (source == null)\n                    throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(source));\n\n                return Task.FromResult(RunInternalAsync(source, cancellationToken));\n            }\n\n            private async IAsyncEnumerable<PigmentFrame> RunInternalAsync(\n                IAsyncEnumerable<PigmentFrame> upstream,\n                [EnumeratorCancellation] CancellationToken cancellationToken)\n            {\n                IAsyncEnumerable<PigmentFrame> current = upstream;\n\n                foreach (var stage in _stages)\n                {\n                    cancellationToken.ThrowIfCancellationRequested();\n\n                    await NotifyStartedAsync(stage, cancellationToken).ConfigureAwait(false);\n\n                    try\n                    {\n                        current = await stage.ProcessAsync(current, cancellationToken)\n                                             .ConfigureAwait(false);\n\n                        await NotifyCompletedAsync(stage, cancellationToken).ConfigureAwait(false);\n                    }\n                    catch (Exception ex)\n                    {\n                        _logger.LogError(ex, \"Stage {StageName} threw an exception.\", stage.Name);\n                        await NotifyErrorAsync(stage, ex, cancellationToken).ConfigureAwait(false);\n                        throw; // Bubble up so compensating transactions can kick in upstream.\n                    }\n                }\n\n                await foreach (var frame in current.WithCancellation(cancellationToken)\n                                                   .ConfigureAwait(false))\n                {\n                    yield return frame;\n                }\n            }\n\n            #region Observer Helpers\n\n            private Task NotifyStartedAsync(IPipelineStage stage, CancellationToken token)\n                => BroadcastAsync(o => o.OnStageStartedAsync(stage.Name, token));\n\n            private Task NotifyCompletedAsync(IPipelineStage stage, CancellationToken token)\n                => BroadcastAsync(o => o.OnStageCompletedAsync(stage.Name, token));\n\n            private Task NotifyErrorAsync(IPipelineStage stage, Exception ex, CancellationToken token)\n                => BroadcastAsync(o => o.OnStageErrorAsync(stage.Name, ex, token));\n\n            private Task BroadcastAsync(Func<ITransformationObserver, Task> action)\n            {\n                if (_observers.Count == 0)\n                    return Task.CompletedTask;\n\n                var tasks = new List<Task>(_observers.Count);\n\n                foreach (var observer in _observers)\n                {\n                    try\n                    {\n                        tasks.Add(action(observer));\n                    }\n                    catch (Exception ex)\n                    {\n                        // Ensure an observer failure does not take down the pipeline.\n                        _logger.LogWarning(ex, \"Observer {ObserverType} threw an exception.\", observer.GetType().Name);\n                    }\n                }\n\n                return Task.WhenAll(tasks);\n            }\n\n            #endregion\n        }\n\n        #endregion\n    }\n\n    #region Example Built-In Stages (For illustration / testability)\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Sample stage that enriches frames by injecting a new column.\n    /// </summary>\n    public sealed class EnrichmentStage : IPipelineStage\n    {\n        private readonly ILogger<EnrichmentStage> _logger;\n\n        public EnrichmentStage(ILogger<EnrichmentStage> logger)\n        {\n            _logger = logger;\n        }\n\n        public string Name => nameof(EnrichmentStage);\n\n        public ValueTask<IAsyncEnumerable<PigmentFrame>> ProcessAsync(IAsyncEnumerable<PigmentFrame> input,\n            CancellationToken cancellationToken = default)\n        {\n            async IAsyncEnumerable<PigmentFrame> Impl(\n                IAsyncEnumerable<PigmentFrame> src,\n                [EnumeratorCancellation] CancellationToken token)\n            {\n                await foreach (var frame in src.WithCancellation(token).ConfigureAwait(false))\n                {\n                    var cols = new Dictionary<string, object?>(frame.Columns)\n                    {\n                        [\"enriched_at\"] = DateTimeOffset.UtcNow\n                    };\n\n                    _logger.LogTrace(\"Frame {FrameId} enriched.\", frame.Id);\n\n                    yield return frame with { Columns = new ReadOnlyDictionary<string, object?>(cols) };\n                }\n            }\n\n            return new ValueTask<IAsyncEnumerable<PigmentFrame>>(Impl(input, cancellationToken));\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Sample quality check stage that validates data rows. Invalid rows are dropped\n    /// and surfaced to monitoring via observer notifications.\n    /// </summary>\n    public sealed class QualityCheckStage : IPipelineStage\n    {\n        private readonly ILogger<QualityCheckStage> _logger;\n\n        public QualityCheckStage(ILogger<QualityCheckStage> logger)\n        {\n            _logger = logger;\n        }\n\n        public string Name => nameof(QualityCheckStage);\n\n        public ValueTask<IAsyncEnumerable<PigmentFrame>> ProcessAsync(\n            IAsyncEnumerable<PigmentFrame> input,\n            CancellationToken cancellationToken = default)\n        {\n            async IAsyncEnumerable<PigmentFrame> Impl(\n                IAsyncEnumerable<PigmentFrame> src,\n                [EnumeratorCancellation] CancellationToken token)\n            {\n                await foreach (var frame in src.WithCancellation(token).ConfigureAwait(false))\n                {\n                    if (!frame.Columns.Any())\n                    {\n                        _logger.LogWarning(\"Frame {FrameId} dropped due to empty schema.\", frame.Id);\n                        continue; // drop\n                    }\n\n                    yield return frame;\n                }\n            }\n\n            return new ValueTask<IAsyncEnumerable<PigmentFrame>>(Impl(input, cancellationToken));\n        }\n    }\n\n    #endregion\n}\n```","numLines":365,"startLine":1,"totalLines":365}}}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-5-20251101","id":"msg_017Ndwt4FT4mXuUw7uZhJSDp","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01USiaZcSa7znw6esQBq1BRV","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/logs/agent/solution.md","content":"# Architectural Analysis and Optimization of Inter-Service Data Transfer\n\n## Executive Summary\n\nThis report analyzes the performance degradation experienced by the PaletteStream ETL Canvas when processing large datasets (>1GB). The root cause is the architectural anti-pattern of embedding large data payloads directly within Kafka events. The recommended solution implements the **Claim Check** enterprise integration pattern to decouple data storage from event notification.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Analysis of Current Data Flow\n\n### 1.1 Data Flow Overview\n\nThe current data flow between the `PaletteStream.Transformer` and `PaletteStream.Quality` services follows this sequence:\n\n```\n[Transformer Service] ---> [Kafka Event (with full payload)] ---> [Quality Service]\n```\n\n### 1.2 Key Classes and Components\n\n#### Transformer Service (`src/Services/PaletteStream.Transformer/`)\n\n**TransformationWorker.cs** (`Core/TransformationWorker.cs:38-178`)\n- The `TransformationWorker` class is a `BackgroundService` that orchestrates the end-to-end transformation lifecycle\n- Key dependencies:\n  - `ITransformationSource _source` - reads raw data envelopes\n  - `ITransformationSink _sink` - writes transformed data and publishes events\n  - `ITransformationStrategyFactory _strategyFactory` - resolves transformation strategies\n  - `IDataQualityService _dataQualityService` - validates data quality\n  - `IReadOnlyCollection<ITransformationObserver> _observers` - lifecycle event observers\n\n- Main execution flow (lines 66-118):\n  1. Reads `TransformationEnvelope` from source\n  2. Resolves and executes transformation strategy\n  3. Validates data quality\n  4. Writes to sink and broadcasts completion events via observers\n\n**Key Data Models** (`Core/TransformationWorker.cs:246-278`):\n- `RawData`: Contains `byte[] Buffer` for raw data\n- `TransformedData`: Contains `byte[] Buffer` for transformed data and `DateTime TransformedUtc`\n- `TransformationEnvelope`: Combines `RawData Payload` and `TransformationMetadata`\n\n#### Quality Service (`src/Services/PaletteStream.Quality/`)\n\n**QualityCheckRunner.cs** (`Core/QualityCheckRunner.cs:135-217`)\n- Executes registered `IQualityCheck` instances against `DataBatch` objects\n- Uses fan-out/fan-in parallelism with configurable concurrency (default 8)\n- Key dependencies:\n  - `IQualityResultSink _resultSink` - persists quality check results\n  - `IEventBus _eventBus` - publishes results to downstream services\n\n**DataBatch Model** (`Core/QualityCheckRunner.cs:18-32`):\n- Contains `Guid Id` and `IReadOnlyDictionary<string, object> Metadata`\n\n#### Shared Event Definitions (`src/Shared/PaletteStream.Shared.Events/`)\n\n**DataEvents.cs** (`DataEvents.cs:156-180`)\n- `DataTransformAppliedEvent`: Published after transformation completes\n  - Contains: `TransformerName`, `Duration`, `OutputRecordCount`\n  - Does NOT contain the actual data payload directly (by design)\n\nHowever, the architectural issue lies in the fact that downstream communication relies on Kafka messaging where the **actual transformed data** gets serialized into the event payload.\n\n#### Kafka Messaging Infrastructure (`src/Shared/PaletteStream.Shared.Messaging/`)\n\n**KafkaProducer.cs** (`KafkaProducer.cs:66-229`)\n- Generic `KafkaProducer<TValue>` with JSON serialization\n- Uses `JsonSerializer.SerializeToUtf8Bytes(data, _options)` for payload serialization (line 220-222)\n- Compression enabled (Snappy by default), but insufficient for large payloads\n\n**KafkaConsumer.cs** (`KafkaConsumer.cs:52-284`)\n- Handles deserialization, error handling, and manual offset commits\n- Deserializes full payload back into memory on consumer side\n\n### 1.3 Kafka Topics Configuration\n\nFrom **Transformer appsettings.json** (`appsettings.json:28-48`):\n```json\n\"Kafka\": {\n  \"BootstrapServers\": \"kafka:9092\",\n  \"Consumer\": {\n    \"Topics\": [\"palettestream.raw.test\"],\n    \"ErrorTopic\": \"palettestream.deadletter.test\"\n  },\n  \"Producer\": {\n    \"CompressionType\": \"gzip\",\n    \"LingerMs\": 20\n  }\n}\n```\n\nFrom **Quality appsettings.json** (`appsettings.json:59-74`):\n```json\n\"Kafka\": {\n  \"Topics\": {\n    \"RawPigments\": \"etl.raw.pigments\",\n    \"DeadLetter\": \"etl.quality.dlq\",\n    \"Success\": \"etl.quality.validated\"\n  }\n}\n```\n\n### 1.4 Current Data Transfer Mechanism\n\nWhen a transformation completes, the `ITransformationSink.WriteAsync()` method (referenced in `TransformationWorker.cs:93`) writes the `TransformedData` which contains:\n- `byte[] Buffer` - the entire transformed dataset\n- `DateTime TransformedUtc` - timestamp\n\nThis data is then serialized and published to Kafka via the `KafkaProducer`, which:\n1. Serializes the entire payload to UTF-8 JSON bytes\n2. Compresses using the configured compression type (gzip/snappy)\n3. Sends to the Kafka broker\n\nThe Quality service's consumer then:\n1. Receives the message from Kafka\n2. Deserializes the full payload into memory\n3. Processes the data batch\n\n---\n\n## 2. Architectural Bottleneck Identification\n\n### 2.1 Why This is an Anti-Pattern\n\nThe current approach of embedding the full data payload within Kafka events violates several architectural principles:\n\n#### 2.1.1 Message Broker Design Constraints\n\n**Kafka Message Size Limits:**\n- Kafka brokers have default `message.max.bytes` of 1MB\n- Even when increased, large messages:\n  - Cause uneven partition distribution\n  - Increase replication overhead\n  - Extend consumer lag recovery time\n\n**Broker Resource Exhaustion:**\n- Kafka brokers hold messages in memory before flushing to disk\n- Large messages directly impact broker heap usage\n- Log segment rotation becomes costly\n\n#### 2.1.2 Network Bandwidth Saturation\n\nWhen processing 1GB+ datasets:\n```\nNetwork Transfer = Raw Payload + Serialization Overhead + Protocol Overhead\n                 = ~1.2-1.5GB per transfer (with JSON serialization)\n```\n\nThis multiplied by:\n- Number of partitions\n- Replication factor (typically 3)\n- Consumer group instances\n\nResults in **3-5x amplification** of network traffic.\n\n#### 2.1.3 Serialization/Deserialization Overhead\n\nThe `JsonAsyncSerializer<T>` in `KafkaProducer.cs:211-226`:\n```csharp\nbyte[] payload = data is null\n    ? Array.Empty<byte>()\n    : JsonSerializer.SerializeToUtf8Bytes(data, _options);\n```\n\nFor 1GB datasets:\n- **Producer Side**: Serialization creates a full copy in memory (~2GB peak usage)\n- **Consumer Side**: Deserialization creates another full copy (~2GB peak usage)\n- **GC Pressure**: Large object heap allocations trigger Gen2 collections\n\n#### 2.1.4 Memory Pressure Analysis\n\n```\nProducer Memory = Original Data + Serialized Copy + Kafka Internal Buffers\n               = 1GB + ~1.2GB + ~256MB = ~2.5GB\n\nConsumer Memory = Received Buffer + Deserialized Object + Processing Copy\n               = ~1.2GB + 1GB + 1GB = ~3.2GB\n```\n\nThis exceeds typical container memory limits and causes:\n- OOM kills\n- Excessive swapping\n- Garbage collection pauses\n\n#### 2.1.5 Consumer Group Rebalancing Impact\n\nWhen a consumer takes too long to process a large message:\n1. Session timeout triggers (`SessionTimeoutMs`: 30000 in config)\n2. Consumer is considered dead\n3. Partition rebalance occurs\n4. Message is reprocessed (duplicating work)\n\n### 2.2 Observed Symptoms\n\nBased on the architecture, the system would exhibit:\n- Kafka producer timeouts (`DeliveryTimeoutMs`: 30000)\n- Consumer lag accumulation\n- Memory-related container restarts\n- Increased p99 latency for quality checks\n- Dead-letter queue overflow\n\n---\n\n## 3. Proposed Optimized Architecture: Claim Check Pattern\n\n### 3.1 Pattern Overview\n\nThe **Claim Check** pattern (also known as Reference-Based Messaging) separates the data payload from the event notification:\n\n```\n[Transformer] ---> [Blob Storage] ---> (Data stored)\n       |\n       └---> [Kafka Event (with URI reference)] ---> [Quality Service]\n                                                           |\n                                                           └---> [Blob Storage] ---> (Data retrieved)\n```\n\n### 3.2 Implementation Details\n\n#### 3.2.1 Service Responsible for Generating the Claim Check\n\nThe **Transformer Service** (`PaletteStream.Transformer`) will be responsible for:\n1. Uploading the transformed data payload to blob storage\n2. Generating a unique data URI (the \"claim check\")\n3. Including this URI in the lightweight Kafka event\n\n#### 3.2.2 Temporary Data Storage Location\n\nThe large data payload will be stored in **Azure Data Lake Storage Gen2**, which is already integrated in the system via `DataLakeClient` (`src/Services/PaletteStream.Loader/DataLake/DataLakeClient.cs`).\n\n**Storage Configuration:**\n```json\n\"DataLake\": {\n  \"ConnectionString\": \"...\",\n  \"Zones\": {\n    \"Staging\": \"azelake/staging/{pipelineId}/{batchId}/\"\n  }\n}\n```\n\nThe data would be stored with this path pattern:\n```\nstaging/transforms/{correlationId}/{timestamp}_{batchId}.parquet\n```\n\n#### 3.2.3 New Lightweight Event Message Content\n\nThe new `DataTransformedEvent` should contain:\n\n```csharp\npublic sealed record DataTransformedEvent : DataEventBase\n{\n    // Existing fields\n    public string TransformerName  { get; init; }\n    public TimeSpan Duration       { get; init; }\n    public int OutputRecordCount   { get; init; }\n\n    // NEW FIELDS for Claim Check\n    public string DataUri          { get; init; }  // e.g., \"adls://staging/transforms/{guid}.parquet\"\n    public long DataSizeBytes      { get; init; }  // For capacity planning\n    public string DataFormat       { get; init; }  // \"parquet\", \"json\", \"csv\"\n    public string DataChecksum     { get; init; }  // SHA256 for integrity verification\n    public DateTimeOffset DataExpiresAt { get; init; } // TTL for cleanup\n}\n```\n\n**Message Size Comparison:**\n- Current: ~1GB+ (full payload)\n- Proposed: ~500 bytes (metadata only)\n\n#### 3.2.4 Quality Service Data Retrieval Process\n\nThe Quality service will be modified to:\n\n1. **Receive the lightweight event** via Kafka consumer\n2. **Extract the Data URI** from the event\n3. **Download the data** from blob storage using `IDataLakeClient`\n4. **Verify integrity** using the checksum\n5. **Process the data batch** as before\n6. **Optionally clean up** or mark for TTL expiration\n\n```csharp\npublic async Task<DataBatch> RetrieveDataBatchAsync(DataTransformedEvent evt, CancellationToken ct)\n{\n    // Download from blob storage\n    using var stream = await _dataLakeClient.DownloadAsync(\n        zone: \"staging\",\n        blobPath: evt.DataUri,\n        cancel: ct);\n\n    // Verify checksum\n    var actualChecksum = ComputeSha256(stream);\n    if (actualChecksum != evt.DataChecksum)\n        throw new DataIntegrityException(\"Checksum mismatch\");\n\n    // Deserialize based on format\n    var data = await DeserializeAsync(stream, evt.DataFormat, ct);\n\n    return new DataBatch(evt.EventId, data);\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## 4. Detailed System Changes Required\n\n### 4.1 Shared Libraries\n\n#### 4.1.1 `PaletteStream.Shared.Events/DataEvents.cs`\n\n**Modify `DataTransformAppliedEvent`** (lines 156-180):\n\n```csharp\npublic sealed record DataTransformAppliedEvent : DataEventBase\n{\n    // Existing fields remain unchanged\n    public string TransformerName  { get; init; }\n    public TimeSpan Duration       { get; init; }\n    public int OutputRecordCount   { get; init; }\n\n    // NEW: Claim Check fields\n    public string DataUri          { get; init; }\n    public long DataSizeBytes      { get; init; }\n    public string DataFormat       { get; init; }\n    public string DataChecksum     { get; init; }\n    public DateTimeOffset DataExpiresAt { get; init; }\n\n    [JsonConstructor]\n    public DataTransformAppliedEvent(\n        // ... existing parameters ...\n        string dataUri,\n        long dataSizeBytes,\n        string dataFormat,\n        string dataChecksum,\n        DateTimeOffset dataExpiresAt,\n        // ... existing parameters ...\n    ) : base(/* ... */)\n    {\n        // ... existing validation ...\n        DataUri = dataUri ?? throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(dataUri));\n        DataSizeBytes = dataSizeBytes >= 0 ? dataSizeBytes : throw new ArgumentOutOfRangeException(nameof(dataSizeBytes));\n        DataFormat = dataFormat ?? throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(dataFormat));\n        DataChecksum = dataChecksum ?? throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(dataChecksum));\n        DataExpiresAt = dataExpiresAt;\n    }\n}\n```\n\n#### 4.1.2 New Shared Interface: `IClaimCheckStorage`\n\nCreate `PaletteStream.Shared.Storage/IClaimCheckStorage.cs`:\n\n```csharp\npublic interface IClaimCheckStorage\n{\n    Task<ClaimCheckResult> StoreAsync(\n        Stream data,\n        string format,\n        Guid correlationId,\n        TimeSpan ttl,\n        CancellationToken ct);\n\n    Task<Stream> RetrieveAsync(\n        string dataUri,\n        CancellationToken ct);\n\n    Task<bool> DeleteAsync(\n        string dataUri,\n        CancellationToken ct);\n}\n\npublic sealed record ClaimCheckResult(\n    string DataUri,\n    long DataSizeBytes,\n    string Checksum,\n    DateTimeOffset ExpiresAt);\n```\n\n### 4.2 Transformer Service\n\n#### 4.2.1 `TransformationWorker.cs` _claimCheckStorage;  // NEW\n\n    public TransformationWorker(\n        // ... existing parameters ...\n        IClaimCheckStorage claimCheckStorage)  // NEW\n    {\n        // ... existing initialization ...\n        _claimCheckStorage = claimCheckStorage ?? throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(claimCheckStorage));\n    }\n}\n```\n\nModify the execution flow (around line claimCheck = await _claimCheckStorage.StoreAsync(\n    data: new MemoryStream(transformed.Buffer),\n    format: \"parquet\",\n    correlationId: envelope.Metadata.EnvelopeId,\n    ttl: TimeSpan.FromHours(24),\n    ct: stoppingToken);\n\n// NEW: Create lightweight event with URI instead of payload\nvar evt = new DataTransformAppliedEvent(\n    // ... existing fields ...\n    dataUri: claimCheck.DataUri,\n    dataSizeBytes: claimCheck.DataSizeBytes,\n    dataFormat: \"parquet\",\n    dataChecksum: claimCheck.Checksum,\n    dataExpiresAt: claimCheck.ExpiresAt);\n\n// Publish lightweight event\nawait _kafkaProducer.ProduceAsync(\"etl.transformed\", evt, stoppingToken);\n```\n\n#### 4.2.2 `ITransformationSink` Interface Update\n\nModify `Core/TransformationWorker.cs` (lines 4.3 Quality Service\n\n#### 4.3.1 `QualityCheckRunner.cs` Modifications\n\nAdd blob storage dependency and retrieval logic:\n\n```csharp\npublic sealed class QualityCheckRunner\n{\n    // Existing claimCheckStorage)  // NEW\n    {\n        // ... existing initialization ...\n        _claimCheckStorage = claimCheckStorage ?? throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(claimCheckStorage));\n    }\n\n dataStream, ct);\n\n        // Run quality checks as before\n        await RunAsync(batch, ct);\n    }\n}\n```\n\n#### 4.3.2 `appsettings.json` Updates\n\nAdd blob storage configuration (matching (Reference Implementation)\n\nThe `DataLakeClient` already exists at `src/Services/PaletteStream.Loader/DataLake/DataLakeClient.cs`. The `IClaimCheckStorage` implementation should wrap this client:\n\n```csharp\npublic sealed class DataLakeClaimCheckStorage : IClaimCheckStorage\n{\n    private readonly IDataLakeClient _dataLakeClient;\n    private readonly DataLakeClaimCheckOptions _options;\n\n    public async Task<ClaimCheckResult> StoreAsync(\n        Stream data,\n        string format,\n        Guid correlationId,\n        TimeSpan ttl,\n        CancellationToken ct)\n    {\n        var path = $\"{_options.PathPrefix}/{correlationId}/{Guid.NewGuid()}.{format}\";\n\n        // Compute checksum\n        var checksum = await ComputeChecksumAsync(data, ct);\n        data.Position = 0;\n\n        // Upload\n        await _dataLakeClient.UploadAsync(\n            zone: _options.Zone,\n            blobPath: path,\n            content: data,\n            overwrite: true,\n            cancel: ct);\n\n        return new ClaimCheckResult(\n            DataUri: $\"adls://{_options.Zone}/{path}\",\n            DataSizeBytes: data.Length,\n            Checksum: checksum,\n            ExpiresAt: DateTimeOffset.UtcNow.Add(ttl));\n    }\n\n    // ... other methods ...\n}\n```\n\n### 4.5 Project File configuration |\n| `src/Services/PaletteStream.Quality/PaletteStream.Quality.csproj` | Modify | Add project reference |\n\n---\n\n## 5. Trade-off Analysis\n\n### 5.1 Advantages\n\n| Aspect | Benefit |\n|--------|---------|\n| **Message Size** | Kafka messages reduced from ~1GB to ~500 bytes (99.99% reduction) |\n| **Network Efficiency** | Eliminates Kafka replication amplification for large payloads |\n| **Memory Usage** | Services no longer need to hold full payloads in memory during messaging |\n| **Scalability** | Kafka can handle millions of lightweight events without broker strain |\n| **Throughput** | Quality service can process events faster, reducing consumer lag |\n| **Failure Recovery** | Failed processing doesn't require re-transmitting large payloads |\n| **Auditability** | Data payloads persist in blob storage for debugging/replay |\n| **Format Flexibility** | Data can be stored in efficient binary formats (Parquet) instead of JSON |\n\n### 5.2 Disadvantages and Mitigations\n\n| Concern | Impact | Mitigation |\n|---------|--------|------------|\n| **Added Complexity** | Two systems to manage (Kafka + Blob) | Abstract behind `IClaimCheckStorage` interface; same pattern as existing `DataLakeClient` |\n| **New Dependency** | Blob storage must be highly available | Azure Data Lake has 99.9% SLA; use with read replicas |\n| **Data Lifecycle** | Orphaned blobs if consumer fails | Implement TTL-based cleanup job; add `DataExpiresAt` field |\n| **Eventual Consistency** | Consumer might try to read before write completes | Ensure `StoreAsync` completes before publishing event; blob storage is strongly consistent |\n| **Extra Latency** | Two network hops instead of one | Blob download adds ~50-200ms; acceptable for batch processing |\n| **Cost** | Additional blob storage costs | Offset by reduced Kafka storage; cheaper per GB; TTL reduces long-term costs |\n| **Security** | Data exposed outside Kafka | Use SAS tokens with short expiry; encrypt at rest; same security as existing Data Lake usage |\n\n### 5.3 Data Lifecycle Management\n\n**Cleanup Strategy:**\n\n1. **TTL-based Expiration**:\n   - Each claim check includes `DataExpiresAt`\n   - Background job scans and deletes expired blobs\n\n2. **Consumer Acknowledgment**:\n   - After successful quality check, consumer can delete the blob\n   - Add flag `DeleteOnSuccess` in configuration\n\n3. **Dead Letter Handling**:\n   - If quality check fails, blob is preserved for debugging\n   - Extend TTL or move to archive zone\n\n**Implementation:**\n\n```csharp\npublic sealed class ClaimCheckCleanupJob : IHostedService\n{\n    private readonly IDataLakeClient _client;\n    private readonly Timer _timer;\n\n    public Task StartAsync(CancellationToken ct)\n    {\n        _timer = new Timer(CleanupExpiredBlobs, null,\n            TimeSpan.FromMinutes(5), TimeSpan.FromMinutes(5));\n        return Task.CompletedTask;\n    }\n\n    private async void CleanupExpiredBlobs(object? state)\n    {\n        // List blobs older than TTL and delete\n        var expired = await _client.ListBlobsAsync(\n            zone: \"staging\",\n            prefix: \"transforms\",\n            olderThan: DateTimeOffset.UtcNow);\n\n        foreach (var blob in expired)\n            await _client.DeleteAsync(blob);\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### 5.4 Fault Tolerance Considerations\n\n| Failure Scenario | Behavior |\n|------------------|----------|\n| Blob upload fails | Transformer retries with Polly; no event published until success |\n| Event publish fails | Blob already stored; can be cleaned up by TTL |\n| Consumer fails after download | Blob persists; message retried from Kafka |\n| Blob download fails | Consumer retries with exponential backoff |\n| Blob already deleted | Consumer receives 404; message goes to dead letter |\n\n---\n\n## 6. Conclusion\n\nThe current architecture's practice of embedding large data payloads within Kafka events is the primary cause of performance degradation for datasets exceeding 1GB. The **Claim Check pattern** provides an elegant solution that:\n\n1. Maintains the event-driven architecture's loose coupling\n2. Reduces Kafka message sizes by 99.99%\n3. Leverages existing Azure Data Lake infrastructure\n4. Introduces manageable additional complexity\n\n**Recommended Next Steps:**\n\n1. The root cause is the architectural anti-pattern of embedding large data payloads directly within Kafka events. The recommended solution implements the **Claim Check** enterprise integration pattern to decouple data storage from event notification.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Analysis of Current Data Flow\n\n### 1.1 Data Flow Overview\n\nThe current data flow between the `PaletteStream.Transformer` and `PaletteStream.Quality` services follows this sequence:\n\n```\n[Transformer Service] ---> [Kafka Event (with full payload)] ---> [Quality Service]\n```\n\n### 1.2 Key Classes and Components\n\n#### Transformer Service (`src/Services/PaletteStream.Transformer/`)\n\n**TransformationWorker.cs** (`Core/TransformationWorker.cs:38-178`)\n- The `TransformationWorker` class is a `BackgroundService` that orchestrates the end-to-end transformation lifecycle\n- Key dependencies:\n  - `ITransformationSource _source` - reads raw data envelopes\n  - `ITransformationSink _sink` - writes transformed data and publishes events\n  - `ITransformationStrategyFactory _strategyFactory` - resolves transformation strategies\n  - `IDataQualityService _dataQualityService` - validates data quality\n  - `IReadOnlyCollection<ITransformationObserver> _observers` - lifecycle event observers\n\n- Main execution flow (lines 66-118):\n  1. Reads `TransformationEnvelope` from source\n  2. Resolves and executes transformation strategy\n  3. Validates data quality\n  4. Writes to sink and broadcasts completion events via observers\n\n**Key Data Models** (`Core/TransformationWorker.cs:246-278`):\n- `RawData`: Contains `byte[] Buffer` for raw data\n- `TransformedData`: Contains `byte[] Buffer` for transformed data and `DateTime TransformedUtc`\n- `TransformationEnvelope`: Combines `RawData Payload` and `TransformationMetadata`\n\n#### Quality Service (`src/Services/PaletteStream.Quality/`)\n\n**QualityCheckRunner.cs** (`Core/QualityCheckRunner.cs:135-217`)\n- Executes registered `IQualityCheck` instances against `DataBatch` objects\n- Uses fan-out/fan-in parallelism with configurable concurrency (default 8)\n- Key dependencies:\n  - `IQualityResultSink _resultSink` - persists quality check results\n  - `IEventBus _eventBus` - publishes results to downstream services\n\n**DataBatch Model** (`Core/QualityCheckRunner.cs:18-32`):\n- Contains `Guid Id` and `IReadOnlyDictionary<string, object> Metadata`\n\n#### Shared Event Definitions (`src/Shared/PaletteStream.Shared.Events/`)\n\n**DataEvents.cs** (`DataEvents.cs:156-180`)\n- `DataTransformAppliedEvent`: Published after transformation completes\n  - Contains: `TransformerName`, `Duration`, `OutputRecordCount`\n  - Does NOT contain the actual data payload directly (by design)\n\nHowever, the architectural issue lies in the fact that downstream communication relies on Kafka messaging where the **actual transformed data** gets serialized into the event payload.\n\n#### Kafka Messaging Infrastructure (`src/Shared/PaletteStream.Shared.Messaging/`)\n\n**KafkaProducer.cs** (`KafkaProducer.cs:66-229`)\n- Generic `KafkaProducer<TValue>` with JSON serialization\n- Uses `JsonSerializer.SerializeToUtf8Bytes(data, _options)` for payload serialization (line 220-222)\n- Compression enabled (Snappy by default), but insufficient for large payloads\n\n**KafkaConsumer.cs** (`KafkaConsumer.cs:52-284`)\n- Handles deserialization, error handling, and manual offset commits\n- Deserializes full payload back into memory on consumer side\n\n### 1.3 Kafka Topics Configuration\n\nFrom **Transformer appsettings.json** (`appsettings.json:28-48`):\n```json\n\"Kafka\": {\n  \"BootstrapServers\": \"kafka:9092\",\n  \"Consumer\": {\n    \"Topics\": [\"palettestream.raw.test\"],\n    \"ErrorTopic\": \"palettestream.deadletter.test\"\n  },\n  \"Producer\": {\n    \"CompressionType\": \"gzip\",\n    \"LingerMs\": 20\n  }\n}\n```\n\nFrom **Quality appsettings.json** (`appsettings.json:59-74`):\n```json\n\"Kafka\": {\n  \"Topics\": {\n    \"RawPigments\": \"etl.raw.pigments\",\n    \"DeadLetter\": \"etl.quality.dlq\",\n    \"Success\": \"etl.quality.validated\"\n  }\n}\n```\n\n### 1.4 Current Data Transfer Mechanism\n\nWhen a transformation completes, the `ITransformationSink.WriteAsync()` method (referenced in `TransformationWorker.cs:93`) writes the `TransformedData` which contains:\n- `byte[] Buffer` - the entire transformed dataset\n- `DateTime TransformedUtc` - timestamp\n\nThis data is then serialized and published to Kafka via the `KafkaProducer`, which:\n1. Serializes the entire payload to UTF-8 JSON bytes\n2. Compresses using the configured compression type (gzip/snappy)\n3. Sends to the Kafka broker\n\nThe Quality service's consumer then:\n1. Receives the message from Kafka\n2. Deserializes the full payload into memory\n3. Processes the data batch\n\n---\n\n## 2. Architectural Bottleneck Identification\n\n### 2.1 Why This is an Anti-Pattern\n\nThe current approach of embedding the full data payload within Kafka events violates several architectural principles:\n\n#### 2.1.1 Message Broker Design Constraints\n\n**Kafka Message Size Limits:**\n- Kafka brokers have default `message.max.bytes` of 1MB\n- Even when increased, large messages:\n  - Cause uneven partition distribution\n  - Increase replication overhead\n  - Extend consumer lag recovery time\n\n**Broker Resource Exhaustion:**\n- Kafka brokers hold messages in memory before flushing to disk\n- Large messages directly impact broker heap usage\n- Log segment rotation becomes costly\n\n#### 2.1.2 Network Bandwidth Saturation\n\nWhen processing 1GB+ datasets:\n```\nNetwork Transfer = Raw Payload + Serialization Overhead + Protocol Overhead\n                 = ~1.2-1.5GB per transfer (with JSON serialization)\n```\n\nThis multiplied by:\n- Number of partitions\n- Replication factor (typically 3)\n- Consumer group instances\n\nResults in **3-5x amplification** of network traffic.\n\n#### 2.1.3 Serialization/Deserialization Overhead\n\nThe `JsonAsyncSerializer<T>` in `KafkaProducer.cs:211-226`:\n```csharp\nbyte[] payload = data is null\n    ? Array.Empty<byte>()\n    : JsonSerializer.SerializeToUtf8Bytes(data, _options);\n```\n\nFor 1GB datasets:\n- **Producer Side**: Serialization creates a full copy in memory (~2GB peak usage)\n- **Consumer Side**: Deserialization creates another full copy (~2GB peak usage)\n- **GC Pressure**: Large object heap allocations trigger Gen2 collections\n\n#### 2.1.4 Memory Pressure Analysis\n\n```\nProducer Memory = Original Data + Serialized Copy + Kafka Internal Buffers\n               = 1GB + ~1.2GB + ~256MB = ~2.5GB\n\nConsumer Memory = Received Buffer + Deserialized Object + Processing Copy\n               = ~1.2GB + 1GB + 1GB = ~3.2GB\n```\n\nThis exceeds typical container memory limits and causes:\n- OOM kills\n- Excessive swapping\n- Garbage collection pauses\n\n#### 2.1.5 Consumer Group Rebalancing Impact\n\nWhen a consumer takes too long to process a large message:\n1. Session timeout triggers (`SessionTimeoutMs`: 30000 in config)\n2. Consumer is considered dead\n3. Partition rebalance occurs\n4. Message is reprocessed (duplicating work)\n\n### 2.2 Observed Symptoms\n\nBased on the architecture, the system would exhibit:\n- Kafka producer timeouts (`DeliveryTimeoutMs`: 30000)\n- Consumer lag accumulation\n- Memory-related container restarts\n- Increased p99 latency for quality checks\n- Dead-letter queue overflow\n\n---\n\n## 3. Proposed Optimized Architecture: Claim Check Pattern\n\n### 3.1 Pattern Overview\n\nThe **Claim Check** pattern (also known as Reference-Based Messaging) separates the data payload from the event notification:\n\n```\n[Transformer] ---> [Blob Storage] ---> (Data stored)\n       |\n       └---> [Kafka Event (with URI reference)] ---> [Quality Service]\n                                                           |\n                                                           └---> [Blob Storage] ---> (Data retrieved)\n```\n\n### 3.2 Implementation Details\n\n#### 3.2.1 Service Responsible for Generating the Claim Check\n\nThe **Transformer Service** (`PaletteStream.Transformer`) will be responsible for:\n1. Uploading the transformed data payload to blob storage\n2. Generating a unique data URI (the \"claim check\")\n3. Including this URI in the lightweight Kafka event\n\n#### 3.2.2 Temporary Data Storage Location\n\nThe large data payload will be stored in **Azure Data Lake Storage Gen2**, which is already integrated in the system via `DataLakeClient` (`src/Services/PaletteStream.Loader/DataLake/DataLakeClient.cs`).\n\n**Storage Configuration:**\n```json\n\"DataLake\": {\n  \"ConnectionString\": \"...\",\n  \"Zones\": {\n    \"Staging\": \"azelake/staging/{pipelineId}/{batchId}/\"\n  }\n}\n```\n\nThe data would be stored with this path pattern:\n```\nstaging/transforms/{correlationId}/{timestamp}_{batchId}.parquet\n```\n\n#### 3.2.3 New Lightweight Event Message Content\n\nThe new `DataTransformedEvent` should contain:\n\n```csharp\npublic sealed record DataTransformedEvent : DataEventBase\n{\n    // Existing fields\n    public string TransformerName  { get; init; }\n    public TimeSpan Duration       { get; init; }\n    public int OutputRecordCount   { get; init; }\n\n    // NEW FIELDS for Claim Check\n    public string DataUri          { get; init; }  // e.g., \"adls://staging/transforms/{guid}.parquet\"\n    public long DataSizeBytes      { get; init; }  // For capacity planning\n    public string DataFormat       { get; init; }  // \"parquet\", \"json\", \"csv\"\n    public string DataChecksum     { get; init; }  // SHA256 for integrity verification\n    public DateTimeOffset DataExpiresAt { get; init; } // TTL for cleanup\n}\n```\n\n**Message Size Comparison:**\n- Current: ~1GB+ (full payload)\n- Proposed: ~500 bytes (metadata only)\n\n#### 3.2.4 Quality Service Data Retrieval Process\n\nThe Quality service will be modified to:\n\n1. **Receive the lightweight event** via Kafka consumer\n2. **Extract the Data URI** from the event\n3. **Download the data** from blob storage using `IDataLakeClient`\n4. **Verify integrity** using the checksum\n5. **Process the data batch** as before\n6. **Optionally clean up** or mark for TTL expiration\n\n```csharp\npublic async Task<DataBatch> RetrieveDataBatchAsync(DataTransformedEvent evt, CancellationToken ct)\n{\n    // Download from blob storage\n    using var stream = await _dataLakeClient.DownloadAsync(\n        zone: \"staging\",\n        blobPath: evt.DataUri,\n        cancel: ct);\n\n    // Verify checksum\n    var actualChecksum = ComputeSha256(stream);\n    if (actualChecksum != evt.DataChecksum)\n        throw new DataIntegrityException(\"Checksum mismatch\");\n\n    // Deserialize based on format\n    var data = await DeserializeAsync(stream, evt.DataFormat, ct);\n\n    return new DataBatch(evt.EventId, data);\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## 4. Detailed System Changes Required\n\n### 4.1 Shared Libraries\n\n#### 4.1.1 `PaletteStream.Shared.Events/DataEvents.cs`\n\n**Modify `DataTransformAppliedEvent`** (lines 156-180):\n\n```csharp\npublic blobPath: path,\n            content: data,\n            overwrite: true,\n            cancel: ct);\n\n        return new ClaimCheckResult(\n            DataUri: $\"adls://{_options.Zone}/{path}\",\n configuration |\n| `src/Services/PaletteStream.Quality/PaletteStream.Quality.csproj` | Modify | Add project reference |\n\n---\n\n## 5. Trade-off Analysis\n\n### 5.1 Advantages\n\n| Aspect | Benefit |\n|--------|---------|\n| **Message Size** | Kafka messages reduced from ~1GB to ~500 bytes (99.99% reduction) |\n| **Network Efficiency** | Eliminates Kafka replication amplification for large payloads |\n| **Memory Usage** | Services no longer need to hold full payloads in memory during messaging |\n| **Scalability** | Kafka can handle millions of lightweight events without broker strain |\n| **Throughput** | Quality service can process events faster, reducing consumer lag |\n| **Failure Recovery** | Failed processing doesn't require re-transmitting large payloads |\n| **Auditability** | Data payloads persist in blob storage for debugging/replay |\n| **Format Flexibility** | Data can be stored in efficient binary formats (Parquet) instead of JSON |\n\n### 5.2 Disadvantages and Mitigations\n\n| Concern | Impact | Mitigation |\n|---------|--------|------------|\n| **Added Complexity** | Two systems to manage (Kafka + Blob) | Abstract behind `IClaimCheckStorage` interface; same pattern as existing `DataLakeClient` |\n| **New Dependency** | Blob storage must be highly available | Azure Data Lake has 99.9% SLA; use with read replicas |\n| **Data Lifecycle** | Orphaned blobs if consumer fails | Implement TTL-based cleanup job; add `DataExpiresAt` field |\n| **Eventual Consistency** | Consumer might try to read before write completes | Ensure `StoreAsync` completes before publishing event; blob storage is strongly consistent |\n| **Extra Latency** | Two network hops instead of one | Blob download adds ~50-200ms; acceptable for batch processing |\n| **Cost** | Additional blob storage costs | Offset by reduced Kafka storage; cheaper per GB; TTL reduces long-term costs |\n| **Security** | Data exposed outside Kafka | Use SAS tokens with short expiry; encrypt at rest; same security as existing Data Lake usage |\n\n### 5.3 Data Lifecycle Management\n\n**Cleanup Strategy:**\n\n1. **TTL-based Expiration**:\n   - Each claim check includes `DataExpiresAt`\n   - Background job scans and deletes expired blobs\n\n2. **Consumer Acknowledgment**:\n   - After successful quality check, consumer can delete the blob\n   - Add flag `DeleteOnSuccess` in configuration\n\n3. **Dead Letter Handling**:\n   - If quality check fails, blob is preserved for debugging\n   - Extend TTL or move to archive zone\n\n**Implementation:**\n\n```csharp\npublic sealed class ClaimCheckCleanupJob : IHostedService\n{\n    private readonly IDataLakeClient _client;\n    private readonly Timer _timer;\n\n    public Task StartAsync(CancellationToken ct)\n    {\n        _timer = new Timer(CleanupExpiredBlobs, null,\n            TimeSpan.FromMinutes(5), TimeSpan.FromMinutes(5));\n        return Task.CompletedTask;\n    }\n\n    private async void CleanupExpiredBlobs(object? state)\n    {\n        // List blobs older than TTL and delete\n        var expired = await _client.ListBlobsAsync(\n            zone: \"staging\",\n            prefix: \"transforms\",\n            olderThan: DateTimeOffset.UtcNow);\n\n        foreach (var blob in expired)\n            await _client.DeleteAsync(blob);\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### 5.4 Fault Tolerance Considerations\n\n| Failure Scenario | Behavior |\n|------------------|----------|\n| Blob upload fails | Transformer retries with Polly; no event published until success |\n| Event publish fails | Blob already stored; can be cleaned up by TTL |\n| Consumer fails after download | Blob persists; message retried from Kafka |\n| Blob download fails | Consumer retries with exponential backoff |\n| Blob already deleted | Consumer receives 404; message goes to dead letter |\n\n---\n\n## 6. Conclusion\n\nThe current architecture's practice of embedding large data payloads within Kafka events is the primary cause of performance degradation for datasets exceeding 1GB. The **Claim Check pattern** provides an elegant solution that:\n\n1. Maintains the event-driven architecture's loose coupling\n2. Reduces Kafka message sizes by 99.99%\n3. Leverages existing Azure Data Lake infrastructure\n4. Introduces manageable additional complexity\n\n**Recommended Next Steps:**\n\n1. Create `PaletteStream.Shared.Storage` project with `IClaimCheckStorage` interface\n2. Implement `DataLakeClaimCheckStorage` using existing `DataLakeClient` patterns\n3. Update `DataTransformAppliedEvent` with claim check fields\n4. Modify `TransformationWorker` to upload data and publish lightweight events\n5. Here's a summary of the findings:\n\n## Summary\n\n### Current Data Flow Analysis\nThe PaletteStream ETL Canvas uses an event-driven microservices architecture with Kafka as the message bus. The key components involved in the data transfer between Transformer and Quality services are:\n\n- **TransformationWorker** (`src/Services/PaletteStream.Transformer/Core/TransformationWorker.cs`) - Background service that orchestrates transformations\n- **QualityCheckRunner** (`src/Services/PaletteStream.Quality/Core/QualityCheckRunner.cs`) - Executes quality checks against data batches\n- **DataTransformAppliedEvent** (`src/Shared/PaletteStream.Shared.Events/DataEvents.cs`) - Event published after transformation\n- **KafkaProducer/KafkaConsumer** (`src/Shared/PaletteStream.Shared.Messaging/`) - Message bus infrastructure\n\n### Identified Bottleneck\nThe core issue is embedding large data payloads directly within Kafka events. This causes:\n- **Message broker strain** - Kafka's default 1MB message limit is exceeded\n- **Network bandwidth saturation** - 3-5x amplification due to replication\n- **Memory pressure** - Serialization creates multiple copies in memory (~2.5GB producer, ~3.2GB consumer)\n- **Consumer rebalancing issues** - Large messages cause session timeouts\n\n### Proposed Solution: Claim Check Pattern\nThe solution separates data storage from event notification:\n\n1. **Transformer Service** uploads transformed data to Azure Data Lake Storage (already in use via `DataLakeClient`)\n2. A lightweight event containing only the **data URI reference** (the \"claim check\") is published to Kafka\n3. **Quality Service** retrieves the data from blob storage using the URI\n\n### Key Changes Required\n- Modify `DataTransformAppliedEvent` to include `DataUri`, `DataSizeBytes`, `DataFormat`, `DataChecksum`, and `DataExpiresAt`\n- Create new `IClaimCheckStorage` interface and implementation\n- Update `TransformationWorker.cs` to upload data and publish lightweight events\n- Update `QualityCheckRunner.cs` to retrieve data using the claim check\n\n### Trade-offs\n**Benefits:**\n- 99.99% reduction in Kafka message size (~500 bytes vs ~1GB)\n- Eliminates memory pressure during messaging\n- Better fault tolerance (data persists in blob storage)\n\n**Considerations:**\n- Added system complexity (mitigated by clean interface abstraction)\n- Data lifecycle management needed (addressed via TTL-based cleanup job)\n- Additional latency for blob download (~50-200ms, acceptable for batch processing)"}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":0,"cache_creation_input_tokens":360,"cache_read_input_tokens":91395,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":360,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":1,"service_tier":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"bcda78b8-a652-4cff-9a5b-a965d192a9be","uuid":"4b93d8c5-55a4-4c08-b00d-b6a2cecd7f44"}
